A Prayer for  

O Jesus, Who as the Good Shepherd of our souls, dwellest in our midst in the Blessed Sacrament, send forth from the tabernacle Thy graces in richest abundance upon Bishop Muench, the 

Spiritual Director of our Apostolate of Suffering. 

Grant him all the graces necessary for his sanctification. Make him watchful over us who have been entrusted to his care. Bless him whenever he raises his heart to Thee in prayer for us; bless him whenever he labors in our behalf; bless him whenever in his sacred office of the priesthood he works for the sanctification and salvation of our souls. 

Make him a shepherd after Thy own heart, living only for his sacred office, so that, when Thou wilt come to judge the shepherds and the flocks, we may be his crown and happiness, and he may with us receive the reward of life everlasting. Amen. 

A Prayer for Our Members 

O Jesus, Divine Good Samaritan, to Thy Sacred Heart we confide all the members of the Apostolate of Suffering. 

Grant us patience, resignation, and a holy love for suffering, so that by the generous acceptance of our trials and sorrows we may promote the extension of Thy Kingdom here on earth and obtain for ourselves an eternal reward.

Deign to alleviate the pains of those who are in extreme agony, bestow the grace of perfect contrition for sin, complete resignation and a happy death upon those who are dying; grant eternal rest to those souls who are detained in the cleansing flames of purgatory.

O Jesus, hear our prayer and grant that not one member of our Apostolate should be lost, but that we who have been united by the bond of suffering here on earth may one day be still more closely united in our heavenly home there to sing the praises of 

Thy Sacred Heart forever. Amen. 

A Prayer for the Seriously Ill 

Most merciful Jesus, Who art the consolation and salvation of all who put their trust in Thee, we humbly beseech Thee by Thy

most bitter passion, grant the recovery of their health to Thy servants, the members of the Apostolate of Suffering, provided this be for their souls' welfare, that with us they may praise and magnify Thy holy name. But if it be Thy holy will to call them out of this world, strengthen them in their last hour, grant them a peaceful death, and life everlasting. Amen.

A Prayer for the Dying 

O Most merciful Jesus, Lover of souls, we beseech Thee by the agony of Thy Sacred Heart and the sorrows of Thy Immaculate 

Mother, purify in Thy Precious Blood all the sick and the sinners who are now in their last agony and who are to die today. May the Precious Blood of Jesus their Redeemer have mercy on them. Amen.

Agonizing Heart of Jesus, once in agony, have mercy on the dying. 

A Prayer for Deceased Members

O God, Whose property it is ever to have mercy and to spare, we humbly beseech Thee in behalf of the souls of Thy servants who were members of the Apostolate of Suffering, and whom Thou hast called out of this world; look down upon them with pity and let them be conducted by the Holy Angels to paradise their true country. Grant that they who believed in Thee and hoped in Thee may not be left to suffer the pains of the purgatorial fire, but may be admitted to eternal joys. Through Jesus 

Christ Our Lord. Amen. 

An Act of Consecration of the Sick to the Sacred Heart of Jesus

O Adorable Heart of Jesus, truly present in the Most Holy Eucharist, Lover of the sick and the afflicted, look down upon me as I offer Thee my loving homage and profound adoration.

In union with all the members of the Apostolate of Suffering, I consecrate myself to Thy Sacred Heart. To Thee I consecrate my body with all its afflictions and infirmities, that I may become a worthy apostle through my life of suffering. Give me the grace to

Prayers 

287 accept my cross of illness in a spirit of humble resignation, repeating Thy own words, "Father, not my will but Thine be done."

To Thee I consecrate all the members of my body. May my eyes look upon sufferings and trials in the light of faith; may my ears always harken to Thy voice which bids me to take up my cross and follow Thee; may my tongue ever speak the words of holy Job, "The Lord hath given, the Lord hath taken, blessed be the Name of the Lord"; may my hands be ever uplifted in prayer for my fellow-men, for my companions in suffering, for my benefactors, and for all the interests of Thy Sacred Heart; may my feet be ever guided toward that way of peace whereon those walk who truly love Thee.

To Thee I consecrate my mind. May I always reflect on the vanity of earthly pleasures, honors, and riches; may I realize that true joy can be found only in the practice of virtue and by living in the shadow of the cross; may my will be submissive and conformative to Thy Holy Will at all times and in every circumstance of life.

To Thee I consecrate my heart. Establish Thy Kingdom firmly therein, and give increase to Thy grace so that I may bring forth fruits of patience and resignation, joy and holiness, love and peace.

To the clemency of Thy divine Heart I commend all the souls who have not experienced the joy of suffering for Thee, and who, questioning Thy ways turn against Thee. Have mercy on them and draw them to Thy loving Heart. Make known to them that 

Thy yoke is sweet and Thy burden light to those who love Thee.

O Eucharistic Heart of Jesus, deign to accept this my consecration; look upon me with love and compassion. Pour down upon me the blessings of Thy Sacred Heart, and give me the grace of perseverance so that having glorified Thee by serving loyally beneath the standard of the cross, I may one day rejoice with Thee in the Kingdom of heaven. Amen.

A Prayer to the Sacred Passion of Christ 

Passion of Christ, comfort me! Comfort me, for the day is long and weary; comfort me as I find my way up the path of life safe to the haven of Thy Sacred Heart; comfort me in sorrow, in pain, in sickness. Comfort me when temptation rages round me and

every hope seems lost. And, when that last dread hour has sounded and my eyes are closing on this world of sin, O, Passion of Christ! comfort me then, and lead me gently to Thy wounded 

Sacred Feet above. Amen. -Rev. Wm. J. Doyle, S.J.

A Prayer for Bishops 

O God, the Shepherd and ruler of all the faithful, look down favorably upon Thy servants whom Thou hast been pleased to appoint shepherds over Thy church. Grant we beseech Thee that they may serve by word and example those over whom they are set, and so attain to eternal life with the flocks committed to their care. Through Christ Our Lord. Amen. 

A Prayer for Priests

O Jesus, Eternal Priest, keep these Thy servants within the shelter of Thy Sacred Heart where none may harm them.

Keep unstained their anointed hands which daily touch Thy 

Sacred Body. 

Keep unsullied their lips purpled with Thy Precious Blood. 

Keep pure and unearthly their hearts sealed with the sublime mark of Thy glorious priesthood. 

Let Thy holy love surround them and shield them from the world's contagion.

Bless their labors with abundant fruit and may they to whom they have ministered be here on earth their joy and consolation and in heaven their beautiful and everlasting crown. Amen. 

- Author Unknown.

Masses throughout the world, as a petition for graces for all seminarians, Thy future priests.

Give them humility, meekness, prudence, and a burning zeal for souls. Fill their hearts with the gifts of the Holy Ghost. Teach them to know and love the Church that they may always and everywhere speak, act, and think with her, the glorious Spouse of Christ.

Prayers to St. Lidwina 

- From the Missal. 

Dear St. Lidwina, who for a long period of years didst suffer intense pain and agony and who hast given us an example of perfect resignation to the Divine Will; obtain for us through thy intercession the grace that by patient and cheerful endurance of our sufferings in union with those of the passion of our Lord and 

Master, we may attain eternal happiness. Amen.

O God, Who hast chosen St. Lidwina, Virgin, to be a model of extraordinary patience and love, grant, we beseech Thee, that we, who with her example and through her intercession have borne the trials of this life according to Thy holy will, may obtain the eternal joys of heaven, through Christ Our Lord. Amen. 

O Lord, our God, Who hast preserved St. Lidwina from the vanities of this world, and hast taught her to prefer with all her heart the sufferings of the Cross, grant us by her example and her merits, to learn to tread under foot the perishable delights of this world, and to surmount by the love of Thy Cross, all our adversities. Who livest and reignest world without end. Amen.

Aspirations 

St. Lidwina, already as a child devoted to the Blessed Virgin, obtain for us a filial love for her. 

St. Lidwina, offering thyself as a victim of reparation for sinners, for priests, and for the souls in purgatory, help us to imitate thy example.

St. Lidwina, unable to take any nourishment for many years, obtain for us the spirit of mortification. 

St. Lidwina, enduring fevers without complaint, help us to accept heat and cold as coming from the hands of God. 

St. Lidwina, devoid of all consolation and plunged into a state of desolation, obtain for use the grace to be faithful in spiritual dryness.

St. Lidwina, poor, and yet trying to alleviate the sufferings of the lowest, fill us with true compassion for the needy. 

St. Lidwina, in the midst of thy sufferings seeking to comfort the sorrowful, arouse in us heartfelt sympathy for the afflicted. 

St. Lidwina, ardently desiring to be united with Jesus in Holy 

Communion, fill us with a relish for this heavenly food. 

St. Lidwina, patient and resigned in the most painful maladies, obtain for us the grace to be faithful to our vocation of being Apostles for Christ through suffering.

POEMS 

According to the multitude of my sorrows in my heart, Thy comforts have given joy to my soul.”

To a Sufferer - Ps. 93:19. 

I pray that you may learn each year to find

Within your fields whence summer joys have flown, The golden wheat of suffering well grown 

And ready to be garnered for mankind:

And may the millstones of the hours grind 

Your precious reaped grain till there alone 

Remain your will powdered within God's Own, And this be still in Love's deep Wounds refined.

Let Mary's hands mold then a gleaming host 

Of this fair flour mingled with her tears 

To paten with her own once sacrificed: 

And let the fire of God, the Holy Ghost, Descending daily burn away your fears 

And leave upon your altar only Christ. 

-A Page, C.S.C. 

291

The Mystery of His Love 

Thou hast chastened, Thou hast scourged me; 

Deep in grief Thou hast submerged me; 

Full of sorrow Thou hast dirged me, - 

O the wonder of Thy Will! 

Thou hast fettered, Thou hast bound me;

In the mesh of fear hast wound me; 

With the thorns of doubt hast crowned me, - 

O the mystery of Thy Ways! 

Thou hast spurned, Thou hast bereft me; 

With the sword of trial hast cleft me;

In the slough of self hast left me, - 

O the splendor of Thy Love! 

- Dr. Francis Pounds. 

A Song from the Sick Room 

Strengthen me, O God, through all my days, To serve my fellow man in little ways. 

Let men of might conceive the mighty towers; 

Let genius brightly deck the world with flowers; 

Enough for me if my rude hearth be warm

When pilgrims come for shelter from the storm. 

No gift I crave to hold a flaming light, But, oh, that I might lead some soul aright;

Might suffer well, might ease another's pain. 

And if I may not swell a chorus strain, Then let me fling a song from my lone hill 

A hymn of highest faith: I know Thy will. 

Strengthen me that I may serve today; 

For serve I must. O God! hear me, I pray!

Poems 

293 

Trusting Him

I have loved Thee, Lord, in sunshine 

When my heart was light and free; 

Shall I bless Thee less in darkness 

When Thy Face I can not see?

Thou through pastures green hast led me 

Where the quiet waters flow, Thou wilt not fail to guide me 

When the storms so wildly blow.

Gladly did I walk beside Thee 

When no cloud obscured the day; 

Shall love prove too weak to follow 

When Thy cross points out the way? 

In the gladness of life's morning 

Heav'nward rose my song to Thee;

Now, the sob which sorrow forces 

Shall my daily offering be. 

I have learned the joy of service, All for Thee, my God, my Love!

In Thy broken Heart there's shelter 

For each poor, storm-beaten dove. 

Midnight lasts awhile, then morning 

When all tears are wiped away;

Now, His Hand may point to Calvary - 

Then, 'twill lead to endless day.

Thoughts for Sufferers 

Let us be minded how precious a thing is suffering: its tiniest drop, clinging at the window-pane of consciousness of trembling on the lashes of memory, may yet be chaliced by intention and lifted by hands grace-sustained to the parched lips of a Lover Divine. 

-A Page, C.S.C. 

Compensation 

There is a peace that cometh after sorrow, Of hope surrendered, not of hope fulfilled; 

A peace that looketh not upon tomorrow,

But calmly on a tempest that is stilled. 

A Peace which lives not now in joy's excesses, Nor in the happy life of love secure;

But in the unerring strength the heart possesses, Of conflicts won while learning to endure. 

A Peace there is in sacrifice secluded; 

A life subdued, from will and passion free, 'Tis not the peace that over Eden brooded, But that which triumphed in Gethsemane.

White Blossoms for God dark hours be dark for me alone 

Nor shadow other lives that I hold dear; 

Let me in laughter cloak each useless moan 

And make my little world a world of cheer. 

Teach me to turn my every hurt and pain 

Into white blossoms of tenderness for Thee, Teach me to make each earthly loss a gain 

And, if I fall, be patient, Lord, with me.

The Wheat and the Grapes 

Just as the wheat in the mill is ground 

Till the grains are one in the Blessed Bread;

Just as the Grapes in the press are bound 

Till their blood in the Blessed Wine runs red;

Our souls must be caught in the Mill of Pain, In the Press of Suffering sacrificed 

Crushed like the grapes-ground like the grain, Till heart and spirit are one with Christ.

GOLDEN MOMENTS 

"In doing good, let us not fail. For in due time we shall reap." 

- Gal. 6:9. 

When illness or infirmity compels people to remain in bed or to recline in a wheel-chair for any length of time, they often find the hours hanging heavily on their hands. Especially is this the case during a long period of convalescence or chronic invalidism. How easy it is to become restless, nervous, and irritable. These hours of enforced idleness can be transformed into golden vessels laden with precious cargo merits. Sent out to sea they will make their way toward the shores of eternity where the priceless treasures they carry will be stored for us. 

Sometimes the sick are at a loss as to the best way of using these precious hours to their spiritual advantage. They tire very easily of reciting the same prayers for the same intentions day after day. This creates monotony, and causes interest to lag. Thus the sick find themselves drifting along with the current of their thoughts. This condition is not always conducive to physical, mental, or spiritual health.

In the columns of "Our Good Samaritan" suggestions have been made from time to time to stimulate their interest, to open the way to new ideas, new thoughts, and new motives whereby they can sanctify not only the idle moments and hours, but entire days and weeks. More than that: they can assist the Church and take an active part in the salvation of souls as well as spreading the kingdom of Christ upon earth. May the following suggestions be an inspiration and an incentive to all who read them. 

1. Be faithful in reciting the morning intention and offering.

Thus the entire day will be spent in the service of God. Renew this intention frequently during the day. 

2. Form the intention of gaining all the indulgences attached to the various prayers you will offer and the good works you will 296

Golden Moments 

297 perform during the day. Have a specific class of souls in purgatory in mind for each day of the month. 

3. Recite the Mass prayers at home when unable to attend Holy 

Mass. Use a daily missal; it will help you to enter into the spirit of the Church according to the various seasons of the year. 

4. Remember to send your guardian angel to Mass in your place. Vary the intentions for which you will offer your Mass prayers. 

5. Be faithful in reciting your rosary every day. Be mindful of the children and the adults who have not been baptized and who are in danger of death. 

6. Pray the stations of the cross every Friday during the year and daily in Lent providing your strength permits. By using a crucifix blessed for that purpose you can gain the same indulgence at home as those who say the stations in church. 

7. Offer a special prayer each day for priests in general, and for your confessor and your parish priests in particular. 

8. Pray for the clergy who are elevated to high honors in the 

Church that they may always retain the spirit of meekness and humility of heart. 

9. Be mindful of the members of the hierarchy, the bishops, archbishops, and cardinals, especially those of your diocese that they may serve by word and example the flock over whom the 

Holy Spirit has placed them. 

10. Pray above all for the Holy Father, the supreme Pontiff, who bears the sorrows and troubles of an entire world upon his shoulders.

II. Remember the seminarians who are aspiring to the priesthood. Pray for their sanctification. A pious seminarian will make a good priest. Occasionally give an alms toward a burse erected for the purpose of educating poor but worthy boys to the priesthood. 

12. Invoke the Holy Ghost for those who are in doubt about their vocations that they may have the grace and courage to heed 

God's call. 

13. Use a Mass clock calendar which tells you the hours when 

Holy Mass is being offered in other countries. Thereby you can unite in spirit both day and night with the priests who offer the

Holy Sacrifice "from the rising of the sun to the going down thereof." 

14. Pray for those sick persons who as yet do not possess the true spirit of resignation to God's holy will. 

15. Invoke the aid of St. Joseph for a happy hour of death for the dying, especially for the members of the Apostolate, above all for yourself. Recite the litany in his honor every Wednesday and daily during the month of March for that intention. 

16. Offer the Litany of the Blessed Virgin every Saturday and daily during the month of May for the lukewarm Catholics that they may become fervent. 

17. Recite the Litany of the Sacred Heart on every Friday and daily during the month of June for all the sick who are not but who in reality should be members of the Apostolate of Suffering. 

18. Recite the Litany of the Holy Name daily during the month of January in reparation for blasphemies. 

19. Let your song of praise and gratitude to God find expression in the beautiful words of the Magnificat. Recite this prayer of praise frequently, preferably every afternoon or evening. 

20. Make a profession of your faith each evening by reciting the 

Apostles Creed. 

21. Try to make the Holy Hour each week, preferably on 

Thursday afternoon or Friday. If your strength is limited, divide the hour into two or four periods. 

22. When Benediction of the Blessed Sacrament is given in church, go there in spirit. Ask the Lord to let His blessing penetrate into the solitude of your sickroom. 

23. Recite the De Profundis once a day for the souls in purgatory, especially for those who are most neglected. Remember the deceased priests. 

24. Say the Little Office of the Immaculate Conception on 

Saturdays for those who are in danger of losing their purity. Ask 

Mary to protect them. 

25. When you hear the clock strike send a little greeting of love to the Divine Prisoner in the tabernacle. 

26. During Lent offer an hour of your suffering for the success of the missions held frequently at that season. It is a time of

Golden Moments 

299 grace, but also a time when the evil spirit prowls about seeking to keep sinners from confession. 

27. Be mission-minded. Do not fail to say a prayer each day for the missionaries, their manifold needs, and for the conversion of pagans. 

28. Pray for the conversion of non-catholics. Many of them are eager to come into the church but lack courage to do so. Ask St. 

Paul to help them. 

29. Devote at least fifteen minutes a day to spiritual reading.

This will be a great aid towards attaining holiness and perfection. 

30. Above all be faithful in observing those devotions which 

Holy Mother Church officially recommends to her children. 

31. On this day pray for the orphans, the aged, the sailors at sea, the soldiers in camp, and for all those persons who have not been mentioned specifically in these daily intentions.

Cross of Christ, The, by Rev. Fulgence Meyer, O.F.M.

THE APOSTOLATE OF SUFFERING A Pious Union of the Sick 

Through the perusal of this book, you dear reader, have become acquainted with the Apostolate of Suffering, the Pious Union which has been established for the spiritual welfare and consolation of the sick and the afflicted. You have no doubt come to realize that sickness is a blessing in disguise, and that suffering is a gift and a privilege that Christ bestows upon His dearest friends.

The object of the Apostolate is to unite all sufferers of the wide world in a spiritual union; its purpose is to assure the sick and the afflicted that their lives are not useless, that by the patient bearing of pain they can help to spread the Kingdom of God here on earth. It aims to promote among the members a spirit of love for suffering and a childlike abandonment to God's Holy Will. 

The Apostolate of Suffering was founded on August 24, 1926, and received the hearty approval of the late Most Reverend 

Sebastian G. Messmer, D.D., Archbishop of Milwaukee, on the

Seventh Centenary of the Death of St. Francis of Assisi, October 

4, 1926. 

The Most Rev. Aloisius J. Muench, D.D., Bishop of Fargo, North Dakota, is the Spiritual Director.

The Apostolate of Suffering has been placed under the protection of the Most Sacred Heart of Jesus, and that of Our Lady, Health of the Sick; its patroness is the invalid virgin, St. Lidwina of Schiedam, Holland, who for a period of years suffered excruciating pain and agony.

Membership in the Apostolate is open to the sick, infirm, crippled and defective; to invalids and incurables; to those who are temporarily ill, and to those who endure some form of physical suffering even though they are able to work. 

Members are asked to offer their sufferings for the following intentions: The conversion of sinners; the salvation of the dying; the 304

The Apostolate of Suffering 

305 Poor Souls in Purgatory, vocations to the priesthood and the religious life; the success of missionary work; the sanctification of the clergy; and in reparation for the sins committed against the 

Blessed Sacrament. 

An annual voluntary contribution of twenty-five cents a year entitles to membership. This small amount is used as stipends for the Holy Masses that are offered for the members, and for defraying the expenses of postage, printing, and the like. 

A voluntary contribution of $10.00 entitles to a Life Membership.

Conditions of Membership 

1. The firm resolution to accept all suffering in harmony with the holy designs of God. 

2. To bear them in a spirit of Christian resignation. 

3. To offer them for the intentions of the Apostolate. This does not exclude other personal intentions. 

4. To send name, address, and dues to the office.

A certificate containing the prescribed daily prayer is given to each member upon enrollment.

The official quarterly publication "Our Good Samaritan,” is sent to each member. It contains instructive and interesting reading matter, and also the dates on which the Holy Masses are offered for them.

Spiritual Benefits 

1. Seventy Holy Masses are offered every month for the members and their intentions. 

2. Also a Holy Mass on all the Holy Days of Obligation, the feasts of the Apostles, Corpus Christi, the Feast of the Most Sacred 

Heart of Jesus, and on the feasts of the following saints: St. 

Joseph, St. Lidwina, the patroness of the Apostolate, St. Camillus, the Seven Dolors of Mary, St. Francis of Assisi, and Our Lady, Health of the Sick. One Holy Mass is offered in the month of 

November for all the deceased members of the Apostolate. 

3. All members share in the prayers of one another, and in the merits of the good works performed in the spirit of the Apostolate.

4. All members are given a “Memento" in the daily Holy Mass of the Spiritual Director and three hundred other priests. 

5. They are remembered in the Divine Office of over four hundred priests. 

6. The members share in an Annual Novena of Holy Masses and in the various other Novenas held during the year. 

7. In the hour of death. they may gain a plenary indulgence through the kind favor of Our Holy Father, Pope Pius XI, who bestowed a special blessing upon all members. 

Those who wish to become members are invited to write to 

THE APOSTOLATE OF SUFFERING, 

Miss Clara M. Tiry, 1551 North 34th Street, Milwaukee, Wisconsin.

This book should be returned to the Library on or before the last date stamped below.

A fine of five cents a day is incurred by retaining it beyond the specified time. 

Please return promptly.
